# Draazy E2E — Coverage Traceability Matrix

Audit of every app route / feature (`frontend/src/App.jsx` + `docs/flows/`) against the
Playwright specs in `tests/`. Status legend:

- ✅ **Covered** — a dedicated spec exercises the feature's key buttons/functions.
- 🟡 **Partial** — touched incidentally or only one surface; needs a dedicated/deeper spec.
- ❌ **Missing** — no spec; **coverage gap to close**.
- ⚠️ **Gap** — was covered, and is not any more. The claim is still true and worth guarding; the spec that guarded it was retired for a reason recorded in the row.
- ⬜ **Retired** — the claim itself no longer has a subject: the branch, build mode or
  screen it was about is gone. Nothing to re-cover.

Suite size: **353 spec files**, grouped audience → feature area under `tests/`:

| Folder | Specs | | Folder | Specs |
|---|---:|---|---|---:|
| `consumer/flatmates` | 52 | | `consumer/property` | 31 |
| `mobile` | 44 | | `consumer/list-property` | 25 |
| `admin` | 39 | | `consumer/account` | 23 |
| `consumer/search` | 28 | | `consumer/services` | 16 |
| `platform` (+`auth`, `help`) | 30 | | `consumer/society` | 12 |
| `consumer/dashboard` | 1 | | `ops` | 12 |
| | | | `consumer/home` | 8 |

Counts are re-derived from the tree, not maintained by hand. The 32 loose specs at `tests/` and `tests/consumer/` are cross-cutting and belong to no one area.

Spec citations below are paths under `tests/`, minus the `.spec.js`; a spec with an unambiguous
name may be cited by that name alone. `scripts/check-coverage-citations.mjs` fails if one of them
stops existing. It validates both forms, but the bare form only inside the spec column. If you add a citation style it does not recognise, teach it the style rather than relying on it to stay quiet.

The shutter is located by `data-testid="capture-button"`, and must stay that way. Its label changes
per step ("Take the front" / "Take the back" / "Take the selfie") and the retake link beneath it
reads "Retake the front" — which *contains* "take the front", so a `getByRole` name regex matches
both controls and every assertion dies of a strict-mode violation.

The face model is the only WASM the app compiles in the browser; the CSP that permits it is guarded
by `frontend/scripts/check-csp.mjs`, because the header that ships to users is a different file from
the one the dev server serves.

**Ephemeral probes, not durable regression coverage**: native Chromium fake-media capture
reaches selfie for all three document types; PAN review/prepared images/retake and camera teardown
pass. Aadhaar and driving-licence require back capture and release streams on exit. WebKit desktop
QR and iPhone-emulated consent/denial UI pass, the latter with an injected denial API because this
Windows WebKit context has no native `mediaDevices`. Physical Android/iPhone remain untested.

**Open failures:** Retry camera does not request a new stream after denial. The five passing specs therefore do not establish
production-ready verification. Details and reproducible steps are in [the worklog](../tasks/todo.md).

## Viewport projects

The suite is **folder-routed** across viewport projects in *both* configs, because behaviours are viewport-dependent and a desktop-only run
would silently pass against a broken phone layout. A spec's folder is a
functional choice: it decides which viewports ever exercise it.

`tests/mobile/**` runs in the **live** suite; the three
projects that matter live in `playwright.config.js` (the live suite, since the mock provider was
deleted):

| Project (default config) | Device | Runs |
|---|---|---|
| `chromium` | Desktop Chrome | every spec **except** `tests/mobile/` |
| `mobile` | Pixel 7 (412×915) | `tests/mobile/home-featured-first` and `home-flatmates-tile` + the converted cross-viewport specs |
| `mobile-small` | 360×640, `hasTouch` | all of `tests/mobile/` |

`playwright.nobackend.config.js` keeps a single `chromium` project; its `mobile-small` and `mobile` projects were removed because they matched zero specs, and a project that matches nothing reports nothing.

`CROSS_VIEWPORT` is the explicit opt-in for specs that assert something genuinely
viewport-dependent. Adding a spec there doubles its runtime, so a spec that only needed one
viewport should not be on the list. Converting a cross-viewport spec means **moving**
its entry to the live config's `mobile` project, never dropping it: a stale path matches
nothing and reports nothing, so halved coverage is silent. The live config's `mobile`
project takes the two width-sensitive `tests/mobile` specs and the converted cross-viewport list
(`referral-rewards`, `help/centre`, `help/help-urls`, `platform/i18n`). The mock list is **empty**.

`mobile-small` carries every `tests/mobile/` spec at the cramped width where bottom chrome,
tap targets and label wrapping break first; `mobile` repeats only the specs whose fold or
wrapping checks differ at 412×915.

**Traps to remember when writing a cross-viewport spec:**

- *Collapsed chrome.* `Footer.jsx` renders each column as an accordion that starts
  **closed** below `sm`. A spec that clicks a footer link blind passes
  on desktop and fails on a phone — see `revealFooterLink()` in `platform/help/help-urls.spec.js`.
- *Unpainted tap targets.* Controls carrying `.tap-extend` are drawn smaller than 44px
  on purpose and restore the touch floor with a transparent 44px `::before`
  (`index.css`). `boundingBox()` measures the painted box, so asserting on it fails a compliant control. `mobile/tap-targets.spec.js` **unions the element box with its
  `::before`/`::after` geometry**, so deleting the
  pseudo-element from `index.css` fails the sweep. A single-element spec can take the cheap route (see
  `mobile/home-taps.spec.js`), but never assert on `boundingBox()` alone.
- *Sweeps that measure a blank page.* `networkidle` fires ~350ms **before** React paints, so a sweep gated on it measures an empty document and passes vacuously. `tap-targets.spec.js` gates on `appReady()` plus a `MIN_CANDIDATES` floor asserted again
  at measurement time. Any new sweep-style spec must do the same.

Deliberately not a coalesce: unstated is reported as unstated, never read as zero. | **Size and deposit are filterable on rent.** Carpet area was gated in three places — the control in `BuyExtraSections`, the URL round-trip in the buy branch of `filtersToParams`/`paramsToFilters`, and `facetQuery` wrapping `minArea`/`maxArea` in `isBuy` — so relaxing any one alone yields a slider that filters nothing; the spec asserts the **wire**, not just the count. `deposit` is nullable and 9 of the 26 approved rentals leave it blank, so the predicate admits NULL and `unstatedFiltered` counts them out, as for age and floor. Area is never null on a rental, so the same page must show **no** disclosure — proving the count is computed per question asked. A hand-edited `?deposit=` on a sale link must not reach the wire | search-listings | consumer/search/rent-range-parity | ✅ |
| **Legacy posted-by URLs are ignored.** Draazy is owner-only, so a stale `?postedBy=agent` link must not paint a chip, must not survive the URL cleanup, and must not send `postedBy` to the public search API. | **Maker-checker visibility across every public read.** One clone per state and deal (pending, needs info, rejected, flagged, paused, sold, rented, archived, approved-but-archived, live re-check, suspended owner), cloned from a reel-eligible live listing so a missing predicate would surface it everywhere. None may come back from `/properties` (including `status=`, `owner=`, `ids=`), `/cards`, `/compare`, `/similar`, `/reels` or `/featured`; `/search-index` carries no ids, so its per-locality count is held to the search total. Browsers may still hold their copy for the rest of `max-age`. A read that overlaps such a write is served but not stored, so it cannot put the pre-write body back for another TTL | search-listings | backend `PublicReadCacheFilterTest` (e2e runs with the cache off) | ✅ |

Mock: duty matches the IGR Leave & License calculator (nearest rupee, ₹100 floor) and charges show paise: ₹20,000 × 11 months on ₹1.5 lakh reads ₹588.00 with a "0.25% works out to ₹587.50" line and no select; 22 months at 5 % reads ₹1,203.00 (₹1,202.50), ₹1,200.00 at every 12 months, and a ₹300 DHC line. The pay CTA reads `Pay ₹x.xx & Submit`. Live: the server's `amount` for the same terms at 5 % is exactly ₹100 above 0 %. Only `clock.pauseAt()` stops `setTimeout` firing, making the flush the *sole* way the draft reaches disk — mutation-verified red (`Received string: ""`) with the flush removed. The clock is **resumed** before the sign-in half, because a paused clock starves a fresh page load of its boot timers. A party who approved loses *Request changes* (hidden; the API answers 409), while the party yet to approve keeps it. Inline-OTP collection shows only to the requester. | **Filed as a valuation, deliberately.** Rent-agreement is the one priced desk, so it opens at `awaiting-payment`, and `ServiceRequestStatus.ALLOWED` lets that reach only `new` or `cancelled`: `STAFF_SETTABLE` excludes `new` so a status endpoint cannot skip the till, and only the signed webhook moves it. A rent-agreement request therefore cannot reach `draft-shared` through the API alone; `ops/registration-check` is the one spec that settles it in Postgres. Valuation is free, starts at `new`, and renders through the *same* `ServiceTracker`; `ops/drafting-desk` files one for the same reason. Live requests sort above closed ones, completed and cancelled both fold behind one "Show N past requests" toggle, and with nothing filed the tracker is not rendered. On `/services/rent-agreement` the wizard's Back/Next row sits in flow at the end of the form (not sticky, so the iOS keyboard cannot float it mid-screen), Ask Draaz never covers Next, and on a phone the hero opens with the breadcrumb. The API's `cancellable` flag hides it from co-fill parties and paid requests. Fixtures are created **over the wire** because seeded rows are raw SQL bypassing `duplicates.reindex(p)` (all 123 seeded properties have a null `address_key`). | **Picking a society fills Locality from its pin** when the society row has none (a fresh mint, a Google place): the place's own area name, else a reverse-geocode of the society's pin, snapped to a canonical locality. Only blank fields or those written by an earlier society pick are filled. **A locality that is not yet a saved row is resolved through Google**: when no live locality matches the society's area name, the wizard asks Places for that exact name, reads its details and posts `POST /localities/resolve`, so a society in a brand-new area binds the freshly minted locality (the spec checks `GET /localities/search` returns it). **A typed society added before the pin is placed carries no coordinates** (the unplaced pin is the Baner default) | list-property-wizard | consumer/list-property/geocode (`picking a Google Maps society fills the locality from its place`, `adding a typed society before placing the pin leaves the pin and locality unset`, `a society picked in an area that is not yet a saved locality resolves that locality through Google and binds it`) | ✅ |
| **A furniture / amenity tile is a control, not a `<div onClick>`.** `FeatureSelector` tiles had no role, `tabIndex` or pressed state: unreachable by keyboard, unannounced by screen readers, and skipped by the app-wide press response (`@media (hover: none)`, which selects `button, [role="button"], [role="switch"], …`). The assertion is a **key press** (a click test passes all states): focus a predefined tile, press Space, demand `aria-pressed` flip with the `checked` class, then Enter to flip it back. Space calls `preventDefault`, so the spec pins `window.scrollY` against a baseline taken *after* `focus()`. Custom entries are asserted separately: activating one *removes* it, so it is named `Remove <label>` rather than carrying a pressed state. The same `<div onClick>` pattern survives in `services/rent-agreement/StepTerms.jsx`, deliberately out of scope | list-property-wizard | consumer/list-property/custom-features (`Furniture: a tile is a real control — keyboard-operable and state-announced`) | ✅ |
| **The Enter that confirms an IME candidate adds nothing.** A Hindi or Marathi IME user's confirming Enter also reaches `onKeyDown`, which used to add the half-typed custom furniture/amenity item. Every free-text Enter handler (custom items, chat composers, smart search, comboboxes) goes through `lib/isImeComposing.js`, which also checks `keyCode === 229` because Safari fires `compositionend` *before* the confirming keydown. The spec dispatches both shapes (Chrome ignores `keyCode` in `KeyboardEventInit`, so the Safari one is a getter) and then demands a plain Enter still adds the item | list-property-wizard | consumer/list-property/custom-features (`Furniture: the Enter that confirms an IME candidate does not add the half-typed item`, `Amenities: a user can add a custom amenity not in our list`) | ✅ |

Also: a farm is no longer asked **length × width in feet** beside an area in guntha or acre (approach road stays); the strength meter no longer scores a **MahaRERA number** on an open-plot sale (optional) nor the layout name. The load-bearing negative is the type switch: `construction` was not in `TYPE_SPECIFIC_KEYS`, so a flat's **possession** answer survived into a plot and published a claim the land form renders no control for. Driven through the wizard because the claim is about what the form lets an owner submit; the wire shape of a published parcel stays with `consumer/property/land-listing`. | **A selected listing gallery is uploaded in one multipart request rather than one request per photo.** Browser coverage counts `POST /me/photos/batch` for a ten-photo selection, confirms a second selection makes just one more request, and keeps the 44px remove target while checking its visible red disc is 22px. | list-property-wizard | upload-policy (`wizard caps a batch at ten, keeps the cap across picks and frees a removed slot`) | ✅ |
| **No property photo is refused for its size; compressing it is the product's job.** `image.worker.js` used to make one bounded attempt (2,560px, quality 0.95, floor 0.9025) and `prepareUpload.js` refused anything still over 1 MB. Now quality is spent first down a six-rung ladder (0.92 → 0.35) so an ordinary shot keeps every pixel, and only a frame that still will not fit sheds resolution, stepped by the ratio the last round *measured* (JPEG bytes track pixel count) down to a 320px floor, so fitting is a property of the loop. `browser-image-compression` is dropped; the native `convertToBlob` ladder also drops the intermediate PNG re-encode and releases the decoded bitmap before the first encode. **The same worker serves documents, where the refusal is kept on purpose**: a scan ladders only to 0.62 and 1,600px. The spec uses two hostile full-RGB-noise originals, one under the 2,560px cap and one at it, and pins the trade-off **conditionally on measured bytes**: the frame stays whole exactly when its own quality-0.35 encode fits, and must be smaller when it does not, with `[true, false]` asserted on that predicate across the pair (neither always-downscale nor never-downscale can pass). Each original is also put through as a document, which must come back at 1,600px or better or not at all. The 25 MB and 48 MP guards stay (they bound decode memory) with their own rejection tests but are no longer quoted in the picker guidance | list-property-wizard, documents | upload-policy (`never refuses a photo for its size: quality is spent before resolution`, `compresses an oversized image without spending resolution it does not have to`, `rejects disallowed formats, disguised files and excessive inputs`) | ✅ |

The wizard no longer asks for the electricity consumer number (the uploaded bill carries it), so the own-duplicate guard keys new listings on the normalised address and photo hashes | list-property-wizard | consumer/list-property/seam-write | ✅ |

| **A digitally signed PDF reaches the vault byte-for-byte** (D228): an Index II or MSEDCL bill is trusted because the issuer signed it, and a re-encode breaks the signature while still producing a document that opens. `pdf.worker.js` forwards a PDF under the 1 MB ceiling unchanged and only reaches `rejectSignatureFields` on the re-save path, so the order of those branches is the whole guarantee. The spec builds a PDF with a real `/Sig` dictionary, `ByteRange` and `AcroForm`, posts it through the wizard, and compares the signed-URL readback with the issued bytes; the fixture's size is asserted too (one over the exclusive ceiling would take the compression path). It also pins that the picker is used only once the photo queue has drained and then shows the filename | list-property-wizard, documents | consumer/list-property/seam-write (`a small signature-bearing PDF is stored byte-for-byte without a re-save`) | ✅ |

Phone/desktop same-row geometry, 44px targets, native-share room URL, property badge styling and room Documents navigation are asserted. 'Needs info - Respond' is raised only for a pending listing in needs-info review; other unread staff messages remain neutral. A finally rejected card (duplicate, broker, RERA, other) offers no Edit (409 listing_not_editable), its tracker reads "Not approved" and the CTA is Contact support; a fixable rejection (photos, wrong details, locality, document, name mismatch) shows a red "Changes needed" step and **Edit & resubmit**, and the edit sends the listing back to review | dashboard-listings | consumer/dashboard/card-actions-and-confirm, consumer/flatmates/agreement-evidence-browser | ✅ |

Assertions a mock provider cannot satisfy: the `PUT` is caught on the wire, the row re-read from the API, and a **second browser context** for the same account finds it in both readers (the Home chip rail and the dashboard's `resume-search` card). The cap test asserts the whole ordered set, not just "the oldest is gone". Boundary: an anonymous visitor's identical search lands in the local rail and issues **no request to the endpoint**. | **The owner privacy switches are account state, not device state (DM).** They used to sit in `dzOwnerPrefs:<mobile>`, so a preference about who may reach you was answered by whichever browser asked. Both now ride `PATCH /auth/me` (`hideNumber`, `verifiedContactOnly`) and come back on every `/auth/me`, `/auth/login` and `/auth/staff-login`. Asserted differently on purpose: `hideNumber` at the UI as a *request* (`postDataJSON().hideNumber === true`), since live mode no longer writes a localStorage key; `verifiedContactOnly` at the API on a throwaway account, because it has teeth (`ContactService.request` throws `VerificationRequiredException`) and flipping it on a fixture actor would change what every other spec gets when asking to reach her. The load-bearing assertion is negative: a counterparty's display name (`Meera Deshpande`) must not appear anywhere in `datasets`; counterparties surface only as an opaque salted `partyRef`. Two tests: a channel toggle is asserted as a `PUT` carrying **all six** required fields (a partial body 422s, and a swallowed 422 looks like success); and quiet hours + match alerts set in one browser are read back in a **second browser context** with its own storage and session, both flipped away from their defaults so a fallback-to-defaults regression cannot pass | — | platform/settings-preferences | ✅ |

| **A customer asks for a refund from Plan & Billing, and the entitlement freezes until staff decide.** `GET /me/payments` replaces the mock history: each row carries its kind, invoice number, status and a `refund` verdict. An unused Contact Pack shows "Refund · until <date>"; asking (Idempotency-Key, replay returns the same row) puts it in Refund pending and freezes its contacts (30 → 15); the desk approves and the row ends Refunded with its credit note. A rejection restores the pack (back to 30), shows "Refund declined" with an "Ask for a review" link, and the same order answers `already_requested`. Reasons read off the row: `not_eligible_used`, `window_closed`; for a Listing Pass `window_not_open` (with the opening date), `pass_no_enquiries`, an enquiry flips it to `not_eligible_used`, `window_closed`, `listing_paused` and `guarantee_used_recently`. Asking freezes the pass and pauses its listing; a rejection resumes it. Another user's order is 404, signed out 401. The signed gateway webhook is machine-local, so invoice and credit-note rows are inserted where it would write them (`helpers/refunds.js seedInvoice/seedCreditNote`); the 5-minute gateway-refund sweep is not run | plans-billing-refer | consumer/services/refund-request | ✅ |
| **Contacts come back.** An owner declining returns the contact (remaining 14 → 15), shows the "Contact returned" chip, sends a `contact.returned` notification, and a re-ask stays declined. A request unanswered past `contactReplyDays` stops counting (backdated `created_at`) and the owner's accept is 409; stored expiry (the daily sweep, emulated by SQL) lets the seeker ask again at the cost of a new contact. `contactPendingMax` (set to 1 through the settings API, restored) refuses the next press with 422 `contact_pending_limit` and the toast, and an answer frees the slot. A paused listing refuses a new request 409 `listing_not_live` ("isn't taking enquiries right now") and the owner's Accept says "Make the listing live to share your number." until it is resumed. A lapsed request reads `expired` from `GET /contacts/status`, so the property page shows the Contact returned chip beside a still-live Request number button | contact-gate-leads | consumer/property/contact-returns | ✅ |

## Mobile chrome & ergonomics

Mobile-only behaviour has no desktop equivalent to piggy-back on, so it gets its own
table rather than a column in the route matrix above.

| Behaviour | Spec(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Bottom tab bar: mount, destinations, Post as a plain centre tab (a dialog opener for the app-wide posting sheet, not a link); the notch and teal bubble sit on the active tab, slide between tabs, take Post before its sheet opens and hide on routes owning no slot | mobile/bottom-nav | ✅ |
| Bottom inset reservation (`.has-bottom-nav` / `--dz-bottom-inset`) | mobile/bottom-inset | ✅ |
| Safe-area insets, `theme-color`, manifest link | mobile/safe-area, mobile/pwa | ✅ |
| **`/listings` platform layer — five declarations that make the page read as an installed app, none visible in a screenshot or reachable from the desktop project.** `interactive-widget=resizes-content` on the viewport meta (else Android Chrome overlays the keyboard and the filter drawer's fixed "Show N results" bar is unreachable). `overscroll-behavior-y: contain` on the **root**, not body (`html` carries `overflow-x: clip`, so body's value never propagates; `usePullToRefresh` skips `preventDefault` once a gesture is uncancellable). The same containment on `.filter-panel` *and* `.filter-scroll` (containing only one still chains the page behind the drawer). `scroll-behavior: auto` under `prefers-reduced-motion` **and** `html.dz-reduce-motion`, asserted on both, with `window.scrollTo` stubbed to assert `goToPage` passes **no** `behavior` (naming one would outrank the stylesheet). The drawer's action bar clearing the home indicator, asserted against the **declared** value via a CSSOM walk matching rules against the element: emulation reports every `env(safe-area-inset-*)` as 0, so computed padding distinguishes nothing. The walk matches *before* recursing, since CSS Nesting gives every plain `CSSStyleRule` an empty `cssRules` and an `else`/`continue` branch swallows the whole sheet | mobile/listings-platform-layer | ✅ |
| **The map detail sheet's three fixes are unasserted** — `86dvh` not `86vh`; `touch-action: pinch-zoom` on `.dz-mdp` with `pan-y pinch-zoom` on `.dz-mdp-scroll` (not `none`, or the sheet stops being magnifiable) plus `data-no-ptr` (else a downward grabber drag arms `usePullToRefresh`); and the grabber dismissing on a downward drag. Reaching `.dz-mdp` at phone width needs the Maps SDK and live pins, and the properties have no signature under emulation; verified on hardware, tracked in `tasks/todo.md` | mobile/listings-platform-layer (documented gap) | 🟡 |
| **`/reels` platform layer — the tells that give a full-screen swipe feed away as a website.** `overscroll-behavior-y: contain` on `.reel-wrap` (an inner scroller; the property does not inherit, so a flick past the last reel rubber-banded the page). The intent filter's `:hover` sits under `@media (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` with an `:active` scale as the press response, asserted as a declaration matched on the scale factor (`:active` cannot be forced headless); chips have a `min-height` for the 44px floor. The rail's `@media (max-height: 700px)` override (44px icons) must come *after* the base `.rail .ic` rule — a media query adds no specificity, so declared before it never applied. `env(safe-area-inset-left/right)` on `.rail`, `.reel-info` and `.reels-top` (landscape under the sensor housing), asserted against the **declared** value via a CSSOM walk keyed on selector text, since `:hover`/`:active` rules match nothing at rest. Emulation reports every inset as 0, so the landscape half is confirmed on a device | mobile/reels-platform-layer | ✅ |

Measured on the toggle buttons and bounded both sides (44 ≤ h < 68: too tall is the regression, too short means the 44px toggle stopped fitting); the expand half is asserted too, so a tile that shrank by not rendering its panel fails | property-detail | mobile/property-density ("the collapsible trust tiles close to a single row") | ✅ |
| **"Sharing this flat?" lands on teams hunting in the listing's locality** — the CTA sent `startGroup`/`title`/`rent`, which nothing read after teams became hunt-only, so it opened the Rooms tab. It opens `/flatmates?view=teams&loc=<locality>`. The split shows exact rent per person (not rounded `₹Nk`) and what 2- and 3-sharing save | property-detail | consumer/property/share-split | ✅ |
| **The detail page's tab strip scrolls on one axis only, and its header opens with the right facts.** `overflow-x: auto` forces `overflow-y: auto` and `.dz-detail-tab`'s `margin-bottom: -1px` gave that axis a few pixels of range, so a thumb aiming at the page wobbled the strip. The lock is `overflow-y: hidden` on `.hscroll-row:not([data-axis-free])` under `@media (pointer: coarse) and (max-width: 639.98px)`: coarse so the active tab's underline cannot clip on a desktop hairline, the width so rails with an `sm:`/`md:overflow-visible` escape hatch are not clipped on a tablet, the opt-out for `Tabs.jsx` (not a scroll container below `sm` on purpose). `touch-action: pan-x` was rejected: it makes the browser discard vertical gestures starting on the rail instead of passing them to the page, which the wheel assertion pins. Asserted as `overflowY === 'hidden'`, NOT `scrollHeight - clientHeight` (an `overflow: hidden` box still reports its overflow region). The header's reading order is covered by the tiered-header row above | property-detail | mobile/property-density | ✅ |
| **The price block quotes only figures the reader acts on.** The header's "EMI starts at" (a flat 0.72% of price from an assumed down payment, rate and tenure the reader never saw) is removed — `PriceInsights` states an EMI further down beside its inputs, and the `/emi-calculator` link stays because a tool is not a quotation. "Rent / sq.ft" is removed for rentals only (a tenant compares a monthly figure; a buyer trades ₹/sq.ft against locality benchmarks). Both rules are one-sided, so the test visits a buy and a rental and asserts the presence half as well as the absence | property-detail | mobile/property-density ("the price block carries only figures the buyer acts on") | ✅ |
| **Zero brokerage is stated once.** Moving the claim into the top trust strip exposed that the page already made it twice — a pill under the price and a bullet in the Draazy Assured panel, which exists not to restate the strip. Counted with `toHaveCount(1)` page-wide, since only a count fails on the duplicate. The chip is deliberately neutral, not emerald: emerald on that strip means Draazy verified something, and no-brokerage is a pricing fact | property-detail | mobile/property-density ("zero brokerage is stated once, in the trust strip") | ✅ |
| **Up-navigation is offered once per viewport.** The teal "Back to results" pill is removed: below `lg` the navbar already carries a back tile (`lg:hidden`) and from `sm` a breadcrumb whose Rent/Buy crumb links to the same place (`hidden sm:flex`), so every viewport keeps a survivor. Both halves are asserted in the project that sees each — the phone spec pins the navbar tile visible, the desktop spec pins the breadcrumb's crumb resolving to a listings `href` — *before* counting the pill's absence, since a page that never rendered satisfies the absence alone. The pill's `navigate(-1)` / `navigate(returnTo, {state:{restore:true}})` behaviour is exactly the navbar tile and the breadcrumb respectively. `property.backToResults`/`backToMap` deleted from all three locales | property-detail | mobile/property-density ("up-navigation is offered once, by the navbar tile"), consumer/property/detail-improvements ("up-navigation is the breadcrumb, and nothing repeats it") | ✅ |
| **Decorative hover states cannot be left stuck on a touchscreen, and the lightbox owns its gesture.** A touchscreen synthesises `:hover` on whatever a finger last landed on until the next tap elsewhere, so an ungated decorative `:hover` cannot be cleared (a tile left glowing, a hero left 5% zoomed, a visit-type `.pick` wearing a paler copy of the `.pick.sel` teal). Fifteen selectors on this page are gated on `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` (keeps the trackpad iPad, excludes Android devices advertising hover they cannot deliver); four more card lifts in the same global sheet — the owner's property rows and the service landing tiles — are asserted from here too, since the CSSOM carries them without loading their routes (`.svc-card` on the services hub is gated but unasserted: it ships in that route's own chunk). Asserted two ways: a CSSOM walk proves each selector still *exists* and is wrapped (a renamed rule would pass as "gated"), and a real `.hover()` on `.detail-card` proves the computed paint does not move (catching a future inline-style or class-toggle hover). The walk guards itself with `seen > 100` and matches before recursing (CSS Nesting gives plain style rules a normally-empty `cssRules` that an `else if` would swallow the sheet into); the touch test first asserts the emulation reports the gate as false. Lightbox: all three `.dz-lightbox` overlays are hand-rolled divs, not `<Modal>`, so none inherits its scroll lock; `touch-action: pinch-zoom` on the overlay stops the photo's 40px swipe panning the listing behind it and a downward swipe firing pull-to-refresh, while keeping pinch for the floor plan (on the overlay because touch-action intersects down the tree, so `none` there would cancel pinch on the image). Arrows are hidden on coarse pointers. Safe-area insets and `dvh` are asserted against the *declared* value via CSSOM (emulation reports insets as 0 and resolves `dvh` like `vh`) and verified on hardware | property-detail | mobile/touch-states | ✅ |
| **The hero is a real scroll container, so the finger drives the photo.** The gallery was one `<img>` whose `src` a `touchend` handler swapped after a 40px drag; it is now a `snap-x snap-mandatory` track of slides. Pinned seams: the container is `overflow-x: auto` with mandatory snapping and `overscroll-x: contain` (a swipe past the last photo must not scroll the listing) plus `overflow-y: hidden` (`overflow-x: auto` alone computes y to `auto`, letting the photo drag vertically; the flatmate room page reuses this track and `consumer/flatmates/detail-page` asserts the same y-lock there); a settled scroll commits `active` (the hidden thumbnails' `aria-current`) on a 120ms settle timer, asserted with auto-retrying matchers — committing mid-drag re-enters the sync effect and yanks the track from a paused finger, hence a `dragging` ref; the reverse direction, a dot tap driving the track, which dies quietly when dots, thumbnails, desktop arrows or the lightbox move `active` without the scroller following; and the request-photos card being the genuine last child. Mid-swipe the dots and counter follow the slide under the finger every frame (a display-only `live` index) while `active` waits for the settle, asserted by holding a touch. Every slide is `scroll-snap-stop: always` so a hard throw rests on the next photo; headless Chromium has no touch fling, so the test uses a three-slide `scrollBy`, which takes the same directional snap and lands on photo 3 without the stop. Above `sm` the track goes `overflow: hidden` and the arrows drive it programmatically. The dots are a labelled group of buttons carrying `aria-current`, not a tablist (`role="tab"` promises an `aria-controls` panel while every slide is in the DOM and swipeable). One dot per photo, capped at the 10 the uploader and `ListingCreate`/`ListingUpdate` accept; the assertions pin the second photo to the second dot, the last photo to the last dot, and that exactly one dot is ever lit (the rail once compared a dot index against `active` under a six-dot cap, leaving every photo past the sixth with a dark rail). Not shown by any emulator: momentum, rubber-banding and 1:1 finger tracking | property-detail | mobile/touch-states | ✅ |
| **Content budget: primary content is reachable, not buried under chrome** | mobile/content-budget | ✅ |
| Assistant coach-mark: never covers the price, budgeted to 2 sightings | mobile/content-budget | ✅ |
| Top-bar scroll behaviour | mobile/topbar-scroll | ✅ |
| Dashboard tabs dock flush at the top edge when the top bar hides on scroll (`dz-docks-under-nav`), leaving no see-through gap | mobile/dashboard-nav ("the dashboard tabs rise to the top edge...") | ✅ |
| Context-aware navbar left slot / account pill | mobile/navbar-context | ✅ |
| Signed-in phone top bar holds 6 visible targets at 360px, not 7 — Compare moves to the account drawer below `lg` and `/compare` stays reachable there (D98b) | mobile/navbar-context ("sheds Compare to the account drawer") | ✅ |
| On-screen keyboard handling in auth | mobile/auth-keyboard | ✅ |
| Bottom nav hides while a text field has the soft keyboard (cookie bar, install prompt and assistant FAB share the same `data-kbd-hide` hook); a shorter viewport with no field focused is not a keyboard | mobile/keyboard-chrome | ✅ |
| Landscape orientation | mobile/landscape | ✅ |
| Space/density optimisation | mobile/space-optimization | ✅ |
| Ops console on a field phone | mobile/ops-field | ✅ |
| Dashboard hub navigation on a phone | mobile/dashboard-hub | ✅ |
| Phase-3 sweep | mobile/phase3 | ✅ |
| Touch fakes `:hover` on first tap and holds it, so every card lift is gated on `@media (hover: hover)` — the query Tailwind's `hoverOnlyWhenSupported` compiles `hover:` utilities behind, so the two never disagree on a hover-capable coarse pointer. Covers the `index.css` rules and the two scoped halves that outrank them, `routes/saved.css` and `routes/listings.css`. The witness is `.cat-card`, not the home Featured card, whose `list-reveal` animation pins its transform and would pass either way | mobile/phase3 ("a tapped card is not left hovering"), platform/desktop-noleak-guardrails ("the card lift survives the hover gate") | ✅ |
| Platform baseline: `touch-action: manipulation` (no double-tap-zoom click delay), `-webkit-text-size-adjust: 100%` (no landscape inflation), `color-scheme: dark` (no light UA chrome on a dark page), `user-select: none` on controls but re-enabled on fields, since `label` wraps its input in ~90 places and inherited `none` costs iOS its long-press Paste | mobile/phase3 ("the platform baseline is declared") | ✅ |
| **Date/time pickers are floating cards below 640px** — every date, time and month field; no native `type="date|time|month"` input is left to open the OS picker (`check-ui-std.mjs` `nativePicker`) | mobile/date-time-fields | ✅ |
| **Searchable dropdowns open as a full-height picker below 640px**, search first and the list ending 12px above the keyboard (or the bottom nav) (`useVisualViewportInsets` → `--dz-vv-top/bottom`), because iOS lays the keyboard over a bottom-docked sheet. The scrim shrinks to a strip, so the search row carries a Cancel exit. Short unsearchable lists are content-height floating cards. The keyboard is simulated by writing the inset; the real one needs a device | mobile/dropdown-picker | ✅ |
| **Dropdowns only offer search from 16 options** (`SEARCH_MIN_OPTIONS`, `components/ui/dropdownSearch.js`, shared by `Select` and `MultiSelect`): shorter lists such as the 8 photo types fit one phone sheet and scroll with no keyboard; localities, floors and amenities keep search; `searchable`/`asyncSearch` still override | consumer/list-property/photo-ux, mobile/dropdown-picker, mobile/wizard-actions | ✅ |

## Shared UI components

Components used across many routes get their own row rather than being implied by
whichever route happens to render them — that is exactly how the picker
stylesheet bug below survived a 178-file suite.

| Component | Spec(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `DateField` / `TimeField` + their dialogs — styled, layered overlay on **every** route | platform/date-time-fields (desktop), mobile/date-time-fields (sheet) | ✅ |
| Ops/consumer forms use `DateField`, not a native date input: lead follow-up, KYC date of birth, registration/submission/issue dates | consumer/account/lead-notes, ops/identity-claim, ops/registration-check, property-ownership-review | ✅ |
| `DualRange` manual entry — Cr/L/K parsing, junk input, Escape, bound ordering | consumer/search/dual-range-parsing | ✅ |
| `useCommitOnRelease` — a slider drag is **one** search, not one per step: nothing is fetched mid-drag, every readout still tracks the thumb, and exactly one `GET /properties` is issued when the value settles. Commit is the native `change` event, not a pointer gesture, so an arrow-key step searches too (an AT slider adjust fires no pointer or key event); because a range fires `change` on *every* keyboard step, holding the key is coalesced to one search. Covers the budget `DualRange` and the near-a-place radius slider | consumer/search/filter-request-volume | ✅ |
| `lib/searchCache.js` — **a search already answered is not asked again**: switching sort away and back re-uses the remembered response and issues **no** request, while a third sort the cache never held still reaches the server (the vacuity guard — "0 requests" is equally consistent with searching switched off). Red-checked at `ttl: 0`. Leaving the route and coming back is deliberately *not* asserted: it costs zero requests with the cache off, so the assertion would stay green with the feature deleted | consumer/search/filter-request-volume | ✅ |
| **Listings request budget on a phone.** A guest `/rent/baner` makes one `GET /bootstrap`, one `GET /properties` and no repeated GET. A signed-in owner going `/listings?deal=buy&bhks=2` → first card → back, 6s later, re-reads neither `/me/listings` (`MyListingsStrip` reuses its read for 30s via `get(..., { maxAge })`) nor `/properties`, and logs no second `POST /demand-signals` for the unchanged search (the intent key outlives the route remount). Red-checked against a bundle with those files at HEAD | consumer/search/listings-request-budget | ✅ |
| **Property page request budget on a phone.** A buyer on a buy listing who opens and closes the contact sheet reads `/contacts/status`, `/me/entitlements` and `/visits` once each: the sheet and close handlers reuse the page's reads for 30s (`REVISIT_MAX_AGE`), and every write still clears the read cache. Hopping to a similar listing and straight back re-reads neither the listing nor its contact gate, and repeats no GET other than `/properties/similar`. The buyer's `/offers/mine` and the owner's `/me/offers` and `/me/finalization-requests` each go once, carrying a UUID `propertyId` (scoped server-side, not a full list filtered client-side). Red-checked at `REVISIT_MAX_AGE = 0` with no `propertyId` scoping: all 3 tests fail | consumer/property/property-request-budget | ✅ |
| **Home request budget on a phone.** The Flatmates tile shows the sum of the move-in, people and teams totals from the `GET /bootstrap` flatmate pulse, with no `GET /flatmates/feed` on Home, and the count matches three separate per-tab reads. Recently viewed reads `/properties/cards` only once scrolled near. Home → a listing → back within 30s reads no flatmate feed, does not re-read `/societies/top` (cached for `PUBLIC_READ_TTL`) and repeats no GET. Red-checked against the previous build: all 3 tests fail | consumer/home/home-request-budget | ✅ |
| **Dashboard request budget on a phone.** An owner returning to the tab (past the 5s reuse window) refreshes every section from one more `GET /me/dashboard` plus `/service-requests`, with no separate `/me/listings`, `/visits`, `/me/*-requests` or flatmate reads. A buyer going `/dashboard` → `/saved` → back re-reads neither `/me/dashboard`, `/service-requests` nor the recommended `/properties` (reused for 30s via `REVISIT_MAX_AGE` / `PUBLIC_READ_TTL`), and repeats no GET. An owner hopping `#billing` → `#properties` → `#billing` → `#properties`, 5.5s apart, reads `/me/entitlements` once (the doc only carries entitlements with `listingTools`, so other tabs don't seed it). Red-checked against the previous build: all 3 tests fail | consumer/dashboard/dashboard-request-budget | ✅ |
| **Flatmates request budget on a phone.** A signed-in buyer who waits past the 5s reuse window, opens a group in-app and comes back reads `/me/flatmate-posts`, `-groups`, `-rooms`, `/me/flatmate-saves/keys` and `/me/flatmate-interests/keys` once each (reused for 30s via `REVISIT_MAX_AGE`; every write still clears the read cache), and repeats no GET. A signed-in cold group detail requests the group while `/me/bootstrap` is still held, so the detail does not wait for the session check. Red-checked against the previous callers: both tests fail | consumer/flatmates/flatmates-request-budget | ✅ |
| **Chat request budget on a phone.** A buyer's cold `/messages` repeats no GET and reads the received request box once: the header's pending-requests badge comes from `/me/bootstrap` (`requestsPending`, then `GET /me/requests/pending-count`), not a second `size=100` received-box read. Sending a message re-reads the thread and `/messages/unread-count` at most once each (the 1s post-send hydrate is skipped when a hydrate already returned the sent message). Hiding and showing the tab within 60s while the message stream is live re-reads none of the unread badges, the pending count nor the inbox (`freshWhileLive`: stream heard within 35s, data under 60s old and read since the last reconnect). Red-checked against the previous build: all 3 tests fail | consumer/account/chat-request-budget | ✅ |
| **List-property request budget on a phone.** A new owner's cold `/list-property?flatmate=1` never reads `/me/listings/quota` (rooms don't use it). On `/list-property` the quota is read once, and toggling Rent → Find a flatmate → Rent out the whole place, even before that read returns, reads it no more. A signed-in cold `?edit=` requests the listing while `/me/bootstrap` is still held (`ListPropertyRoute` preloads the wizard chunk and prefetches the listing behind `ProtectedRoute`), reads it exactly once and repeats no GET. Red-checked with the flatmate skip and the prefetch removed: both tests fail | consumer/list-property/wizard-request-budget | ✅ |
| **Services request budget on a phone.** An owner's cold `/services/rent-agreement` reads `/service-requests`, `/me/service-request-invites`, `/me/listings`, `/me/entitlements` and `/fees` once each with no repeated GET, and going to `/services` and back in-app within 30s makes no GET at all (`REVISIT_READ`; `/fees` is public reference data cached 30s; writes and explicit refreshes still read fresh). Filing a rent agreement uploads its papers (`POST …/docs`) without a `GET /service-requests/{id}` before checkout. Red-checked with the revisit reuse and post-upload re-read restored: both tests fail | consumer/services/services-request-budget | ✅ |
| **Admin request budget.** A cold `/admin` reads `/bootstrap` and `/me/bootstrap` once each (the inline early read is accepted for staff because it skips the HTTP cache) and the help index `GET /admin/help/staff/index` once, never the article bodies at `/admin/help/staff`. A cold `/admin/users` reads `/users` once. The Places Duplicates tab checks its whole queue with one `GET /admin/society-candidates/duplicates?slugs=…` and no per-slug `/{slug}/duplicates`. Red-checked with the full help tree, per-slug reads and a second users load: all 3 tests fail | admin/admin-request-budget | ✅ |
| **Ops request budget.** A rental staffer sent to `/staff/legal` (a desk they can't use) lands on `/staff` with one `GET /admin/settings/flags` (guards redirect to the caller's portal, not `/admin` and back). Opening a valuation case on the drafting desk and a rent-agreement case asks for `/service-requests/{id}/checklist` while the case read is still in flight. A `?request=` deep link to a valuation case opens it with one `GET /service-requests/{id}` (the desk takes the case `useLinkedCase` already read). On a prod build (`E2E_DIST`; skipped on the dev server), a cached reload of `/admin/interior` has no idle stretch of 200ms or more between page module loads before the queue read (`BackOfficeEntry` waits for the page chunk, so React 19's 300ms Suspense fallback hold never starts). Red-checked with the callers reverted: all 5 tests fail (cold desk 4-5 of 5 runs, ~315ms) | admin/ops-request-budget | ✅ |
| `ConnectivityBanner` + `useConnectivity` + `LoadError` — **offline vs unreachable**: `setOffline(true)` raises the confident "You're offline" and coming back announces recovery then clears; a `page.route` abort while `navigator.onLine` stays **true** raises only the hedged "Can't reach the server"; a 500 answers so **no** banner is painted; a failed load offers a Retry instead of an empty state and the retry succeeds once the route is un-aborted; the live region is mounted while empty and announcing does not move focus; a GET the server never answers gives up after 20 s (fake clock) with the unreachable message and a Retry (D165) | consumer/connectivity | ✅ |
| `ErrorBoundary` chunk-load fallback — **a route chunk a deploy deleted heals itself**: online, a failed lazy page import updates the service worker and reloads once, and the page renders; if the chunk still fails after that reload the "This page couldn't load" card stays up with no second reload (no loop); offline it shows the calm offline card instead of the raw import error | consumer/connectivity | ✅ |
| `ConnectivityBanner` + `useAppUpdate` + `BuildStampFilter` — **a tab that outlived its build**: rewriting `X-Draazy-Build` on the second `/api` answer raises "A new version is available" on the *fourth* banner state, pinned as **outranked but not discarded** — going offline with the offer latched swaps the copy to "You're offline" (a reload on a dead uplink lands on a blank page), and the offer returns intact once the recovery notice clears. Nothing reloads by itself: a boot counter in `sessionStorage` (which survives a reload, unlike anything on `window`) stays at **1** across the banner appearing and across a dismiss, because the wizard holds minutes of unsaved typing; and a dismissed build **stays** dismissed across a real in-app navigation that sends more traffic carrying it, since a rolling deploy drains the old version gradually and an unlatched banner would strobe through every cutover. Vacuity is closed by a control that freezes the stamp and asserts **no** banner, plus a hit count that fails loudly if boot ever collapses to the single request the staging trick depends on. A guest `/listings` boot sends only one stamped read (reference reads are cached and unstamped, the page-view beacon waits 60s), so each test makes a client-side search change to send the second | consumer/app-update | ✅ |
| `ArticleFeedback` — positive/negative branches, persistence, per-article reset. The verdict also **leaves the browser**: `POST /help/feedback` carries the slug, the rendered language and `helpful`, claims no `comment` when none was typed, and is accepted (**202**, not merely dispatched). A thumbs-down reports **twice by design** — once bare when clicked, so a reader who never writes a reason still counts, and again with the prose — while the local record waits for the submit, so abandoning leaves the box reachable. The upload is fire-and-forget: aborting the route still thanks the reader and still writes the local record. The per-article reset is reached by **clicking** an in-app link, because a `goto` remounts the widget and would pass with the reset broken | platform/help/article-feedback | ✅ |
| `ToastContext` timing (D365) — a success toast is still up at 4.5 s and gone by 5.5 s (5 s floor; reading time is 1.5 s + 0.4 s/word, capped at 12 s); hovering holds it open past 20 s and leaving restarts the clock; the same toast raised again while held stays held; an error toast is still up after 60 s and closes only on Dismiss. Fake clock, society share with a stubbed clipboard | platform/toast-timing | ✅ |


## Admin

| Route / Feature | Flow doc | Spec(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/admin` **RBAC nav** — which tabs an account may see, including manager atom-gated access, and which shell it may load at all | (all) | admin/consolidation, admin/rbac | ✅ |
| **Grouped admin sidebar** — Dashboard and Runbooks stay pinned; every other tab sits in a dropdown group (Listings & Users, Service Desks, Support, Money, Content & Places, Insights, Administration). The group holding the current page opens on its own, a toggle opens or closes it, and a group with only one visible tab shows that tab directly | (all) | admin/sidebar-groups | ✅ |
| **The `/admin` scorecard is the server's, not the browser's** (D248) — `AdminDashboard.jsx` imported seven read functions from `lib/mockApi.js`, *below* the service seam, so on a live build eleven of fourteen tiles, both activity cards and the platform-health panel showed numbers invented in the browser, and three side panels (Smart Alerts, SLA Health, Daily Ops Scorecard) came from a seeded PRNG with no server counterpart. The screen now reads `GET /admin/dashboard` → `AdminKpis` through `analyticsService.adminDashboard` (its `kpis` section), with assertions the old code could not satisfy. **Totals cannot be reached by paging**: `/users` serves 20 a page against 88 seeded users, so `88` comes only from the aggregate and `20` is what the regression looks like; the spec asserts `totalUsers > 20` first, so the tile still discriminates. **A fallback to mock is a `console.warn`, not an error**, so the mock seed's 62 users / 80 listings would render a confident wrong page; the live figures differ in every asserted field. Two contract drifts caught in the repoint: `traffic[]` rows carry `sessions`, not `visits` (a naive repoint renders `0`), and ticket status `new` does not exist server-side (`open`/`in-progress`/`waiting`/`resolved`/`closed`), which `ticketMapper` deliberately does not translate. `revenue30d` is asserted on both halves of its redaction — non-null for an admin, `null` for staff, every other field identical — because `fmtINR(null)` prints ₹0 | (all) | admin/admin-dashboard | ✅ |
| **An ops account is moved to the staff portal, never served the admin console** — `/admin/properties` lands a staffer on `/staff/properties` (no Settings or Team links), and `/admin` lands her on `/staff` with her "My work" page. `GET /admin/dashboard` redacts per section, so a staffer without the `analytics` function gets no `kpis`/`traffic`/`sla` section (absent, never zero); one holding it is served the scorecard with `revenue30d` null (finance is admin-only) and every other field equal to the admin's. Pinned together: the API is the security boundary and the client guard must already be correct | (all) | admin/admin-dashboard, admin/rbac | ✅ |
| **The `/admin` queues underneath the scorecard are the server's too** (D249) — `AdminDashboard` fetched `listForModeration({}, 'newest')` (a hundred-row cap over the newest listings in every status) and derived the Flagged tile, the stale/awaiting-owner follow-up lists and the Pending verification card from that one array. Every derivation was wrong past the cap, in the direction that hides work: a browser-side `filter(status === 'flagged').length` can only move *downwards* as the catalogue grows, and searching for the listings that have waited *longest* inside a page of the *newest* rows discards the rows the card exists to surface. The queue, its counts (pending, flagged, follow-up) and the five oldest rows now come from the `listings` section of `GET /admin/dashboard` as whole-set COUNT queries; New Enquiries / Deals in Progress had filtered on statuses that do not exist (`new`, `in_progress`) and always read 0 — they now count `pending` contact requests and deals not `closed`. Both claims fail the old code without needing a hundred-listing database: **the queue card's five rows are asserted in order against an independent `GET /admin/properties?status=pending&archived=false&sort=createdAt,asc&size=5`, behind a premise guard that fails unless the ascending and descending heads differ** (no vacuous green on a small seed; `PropertySort` appends `id desc` as a total tiebreaker, so the comparison is deterministic). **The Flagged tile is pinned by where its number comes from, not the number** — inside the page cap the old filter and the server count agree, so the discriminating assertion is that serving `/admin/dashboard` without its `listings` section takes the tile off the page entirely (a screen that zeroed a missing section cannot do that), beside a Pending Verification tile asserted equal to `GET /admin/properties/summary`; the still-present tile stops that passing on a page that failed to render | (all) | admin/admin-dashboard | ✅ |
| **Each `/admin` tile counts the list it opens.** Needs Follow-up equals the Follow-up tab's total (`GET /admin/properties?status=approved&archived=false&unconfirmed=true`), not stale pending listings. Scheduled Visits opens `/admin/enquiries?tab=visits&status=scheduled` with the Scheduled chip pressed; New Enquiries and Deals open their own tabs. | **`/admin/analytics` Pricing and SLA against the live API** — the two tabs that read the server, retiring two lies. `pricingInsight()` filled a locality's missing asking rate with its own curated market rate, so an empty locality scored a deviation of zero and was reported *fairly priced*; `slaMetrics()` drew every approval turnaround from a seeded PRNG. **Pricing:** market and asking rates are separate fields present on every row even when null (an omitted key lets the client restore the fallback with `?? marketRatePerSqft`), `buy + rent == total` exactly (`≤` would pass with both filters broken and returning 0), and every locality that **has** a curated market rate but no priced buy listing reports `null` rather than borrowing it. Only approved **flats** are averaged and a locality needs 3 of them before it gets an asking rate or yield (backend `AdminPricingAnalyticsTest`), so the e2e seed must keep at least one such locality; that is the only population that proves anything. Inequality on measured rows is not asserted, since seed listing prices derive from the locality market rate. **SLA:** `targetHours` pinned at 24, the backlog anchored to more than one row before its longest-wait-first ordering is asserted (otherwise `worstPending: []` satisfies the test), and `?days=0` refused **400**. Plus the **three service tracks** from D252 — Ticket Pickup (4h), Service Delivery (72h) and the Concierge Pipeline (168h), each derived from `audit_log`: targets are pinned (the client colours against them), and the **null contract** carries the row — a track with no completions reports `avgHours`, `medianHours` and `slaRatePct` as `null`, never `0`. Counts are asserted as shape (numbers, never null or negative) with two containment rules (`breachedCount ≤ completedCount`, `outstandingBreachingCount ≤ outstandingCount`), and `slaRatePct` is reconciled against the counts it derives from. **Both** routes are refused **403** to a plain consumer (separate copies of the guard). The two UI tests assert a value only the database holds (a measured locality's exact asking rate and buy count; the title of the oldest listing in the real review queue); on their first run they failed because `analytics` was missing from `VITE_API_DOMAINS` and the tab served the mock provider (see tasks/lessons.md). Counts are otherwise relationships and null contracts, since other live specs create and approve listings during a full run. **Update:** the curated market rate is gone with the locality rework, so the Pricing table is Locality / Asking / Avg rent / Yield / Buy / Rent / Total; the UI step asserts no "market" header and a "—" in the asking column of a locality without enough listings | analytics | admin/analytics | ✅ |

| **One listing, one case file — notes filed from the enquiries board were invisible on the listing they were about.** A listing answers to two identifiers: `propertyMapper` sets the seam id to `p.slug \|\| p.id` (so catalogue screens hold a **slug**), while `enquiryBoardMapper` carries the raw `propertyId`, a **uuid**. `internal_notes.entity_id` is `text` and was stored verbatim, so `AdminEnquiries` filing under the uuid and the review modal reading under the slug were two disjoint histories with no error and both writes 201. Proven by HTTP before the fix: a POST under each id, then a GET under each, one row apiece. Canonicalisation belongs on the server (client discipline had already failed: `PropertyReviewModal` declares `const pid = (listing) => listing?.uuid \|\| listing?.id` and `listNotes` does not use it), following the `ReviewTargetKey` precedent. `NoteEntityKey` resolves a non-uuid property id through `findBySlug` — with no `archived` filter deliberately, because V90 made `entity_id` a bare text column so a note about a listing archived later survives — and `V123` merges the histories already written. **No mock suite could catch this**: each side was self-consistent and one localStorage database answers to whichever id it is handed. The assertion must *cross*: file under the uuid in the enquiries board's shape (`action: 'responded'`), file under the slug in the console's, then require **both texts** back from **both** reads. Mutation-proven by returning the id unresolved. A second test pins that normalising did not become existence-checking: an id matching no listing still takes a note. The comms-log timeline test lives here too, using a **freshly created** listing (no slug at all, so stable across the approval it performs), while the crossing test uses a seeded listing and only adds rows, leaving the queue counts `properties-console` asserts untouched | property-verification | admin/notes | ✅ |
| **The concierge wizard's six steps, split along the line where a server starts to matter** — the mock specs for this screen have been retired (M12, M13; `post-on-behalf-fixes.spec.js` went with them): the deal toggle relabelling pricing, the type cascade clearing a stale BHK before Review prints it, a land type hiding floor/facing/furnishing, the browser-local draft offering Resume after a refresh, and label-to-input association now run on the live build. The **one** thing still read from the browser is `dz_pob_draft_v1`, the key preserving a half-typed form across a refresh: operator-local state the feature owns, so the test clears and reads it back rather than seeding a record with it. `post-on-behalf.spec.js`'s last five (the two routes in, the step-one guard, both grouping scales and both `moneyWords` branches) became one live test (M12) tying the two numbers on screen to the listing read back off `/me/listings`. Mutation-proved from both ends: Western grouping in `formatIndian` reddens the display alone; dividing the submitted price by ten in the wizard's mapper reddens the record alone. A third mutation *failed* to fail: dropping the `.replace(/\D/g, '')` that keeps form state digits-only is inert, because `AdminPostOnBehalf.jsx:214` runs `parseAmount` on the way out. The live half drives all six steps and asks the **owner's own session** what the server stored (the request body is assembled by `toListingCreate`, which renames keys, translates two enums the contract 422s without, and drops one on purpose): amenities by name, and a deposit typed while the deal said rent reading **0** once flipped to sale. The two carry each other's controls: the rent listing asserts its deposit *did* arrive (48,000), and the sale listing ticks nothing and asserts the server supplies no amenities of its own. (2) Floor 5 of 4 is held on the Details step. (3) Ground floor arrives as `floor: 0` with `totalFloors: 4`. (4) Rent and deposit arrive, with no amenities filed. What is left is the redirect and its two guards: an unauthenticated visitor is still sent to `/staff-login` rather than through to the ops desk, and a buyer is still refused. **`flatmate-moderation-reach.spec.js` has since been retired**: it covered the consumer board honouring D72's publication rule, and its docblock described that rule backwards (it claimed the server filters `mod_status not in ('flagged','removed','rejected')` and the mock mirrors it via `MOD_HIDDEN`; in fact `FlatmateRoomRepository` filters `r.modStatus in ('live','approved')`, an **allow-list**, and the frontend export is `MOD_PUBLIC`). Its two absence tests could not fail for the regression the design exists to prevent: a blacklist and a whitelist agree on `flagged`, `removed` and `rejected` and disagree on `pending`, the state every new post is born in. Rewriting `isPubliclyVisible` as the blacklist left both green and reddened only the new third test, `an unreviewed room is hidden` (a leaking `pending` makes every unreviewed post public, silently). ## Ops

| Route / Feature | Flow doc | Spec(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/ops/flatmate-review` — the flatmate desk, live-only. **One queue, four tabs, one popup:** Pending (default, newest first) merges posts/rooms/groups awaiting publish, published rows edited since review and pending group applications into one card list; then **Badge requests** (only open Verified property claims, oldest first; a room awaiting publish and claiming the badge shows once in each tab), Published, and Hidden & removed. Every decision is taken in the **Review flatmate post** popup, which loads `GET /admin/flatmates/{id}` (photos, full untruncated free text, details grid, full host mobile, an **Edit details** form for headline, description, rent/budget, deposit, localities and move-in via `PATCH /admin/flatmates/{id}` (audited, no re-review), re-check, Badge verification, Decision). The old Verification / Moderation / Group applications boards are gone (the mock `lib/data/flatmates.js` knew only host verification, not the D72 publication axis or group applications). Group applications needed a writer: `POST /flatmates/groups/{id}/apply`, an owner inbox (`GET`/`PATCH /me/group-applications`) and `GET /me/flatmate-groups` — four routes the OpenAPI contract does not name, an intentional extension recorded in `docs/flows/ops/flatmate-moderation.md` section 10. Rulings: the host's mobile is shown in full to the back office; the moderation axis offers no `rejected` (it would mean `removed`); the bare word **flag** appears nowhere on the desk (`flag_for_review` is a server guardrail, `mod_status = 'flagged'` this desk's verdict) — the boards say **Hide for review** / **Hidden for review**, verification says **Contested address** (D9). Live coverage (9 tests, one pinning the three-tab layout): a tenant declaration reaches Verification and approving moves the **badge and not the post** (the two axes must not reach each other); a rejection without a reason is refused; a new seeker post is born Pending and absent from the public feed until Publish (D72); Remove takes a note and free text renders untruncated (where a phone number goes when the contact field will not take one); the group-applications board shows a **real** row; the uploaded agreement is openable from its row (a row without one says so); the retired `/admin/flatmates` hands the operator to this desk without laundering a switched-off module onto it (M9); and the **Re-check** board (D5): a published tenant-tier room with every photo replaced *stays up* but lands on its own board naming the field that moved (the edit path re-ran only the trust ladder, which knows who the host is, not what changed), with photo thumbnails on the row (D6). A second room edited in the same run changing only a chip-list facet is asserted **not** there, and deciding the row clears it. The swap is driven over the API (`PATCH /flatmates/rooms/{id}` has no host screen; only `/seats` and `/occupants`); assertions are on the rendered board. Fixtures are minted through the consumer path (the seed's five flatmate rows are all `approved`). M11 deleted the three seeded consumer cues from `flatmate-review.spec.js`: `consumer/flatmates/review-status` owns all three labels more strongly (a group waiting on Ops says so and is refused the badge; an Ops approval turns it Tenant-verified; a rejected group stays on the board saying the review failed — each posted over HTTP, moderated through the real queue, then read off the card, so the label is proved **earned**); the old test planted rows into `draazyFlatmateReviews`, a per-browser `localStorage` map.
