| **Refund with a manager approver** (`Refunds` on the desk, D-b). On a paid request the holder sees the paid amount and both ceilings. A duty-paid ask above the after-duty ceiling returns 422. The holder asks for the full amount in the UI and sees "Waiting for a manager to approve it." with Withdraw but no Approve button; `canDecide` is false for staff, and the maker's own approve returns 403. The admin approves: refunded = paid, a gateway ref is stored, `refund.approved` is on the timeline, and the customer gets 403 on the refunds read. Backend `RentAgreementRefundTest` covers managers and admins being alerted (`service.refund-requested`), staff approve/reject refused (withdraw allowed), the fee-only-after-duty rule, a registration forcing duty-paid with its GRN, the refusals (unpaid, other desk, customer, a second open ask, a bad GRN), a refund of everything paid cancelling the request, and the signed Cashfree `REFUND_STATUS_WEBHOOK` marking a CANCELLED/FAILED refund `failed` (timeline, audit, customer told) while SUCCESS changes nothing. Deciding an already-decided refund is 409 (a racing second approval loses cleanly); an unknown refund id is 404. | service-queues | ops/registration-check ("a refund the holder asks for waits for a manager…") | ✅ |
| **Desk cancel raises the refund** (STAFF-CANCEL-REFUND). Cancelling a paid rent agreement shows a refund note in the cancel dialog; the cancel raises a refund of what is still refundable, the customer's `service.cancelled` carries the reason and "refund is waiting for approval", staff see it with no Withdraw and cannot approve or reject it (403), and an admin opening `/admin/rent-agreement?request=<id>` approves it on the Payment tab (refunded = paid, still cancelled). Backend `RentAgreementRefundTest` (`aDeskCancelRaisesTheRefundForAManager`, `aCancelKeepsTheOpenRefund`, `aManagersOwnCancelTheyCanApprove`, `aCancelledRequestsRefundIsAManagersCall`) covers managers being alerted, an open refund kept rather than duplicated, a cancelling manager approving the refund their own cancel raised, staff refused a withdraw once cancelled (only a manager refuses), and a multi-line reason ending in `!` read without a stray full stop. | service-queues | ops/registration-check ("a desk cancel of a paid agreement raises the refund…") | ✅ |
| **Rent-agreement refunds on the Refunds desk; Cancelled tab** (REFUNDS-FEATURE-FILTER, DESK-CANCELLED-TAB). A desk cancel's refund shows on `/admin/refunds` Pending as "Rent agreement" with the customer's mobile, the cancel reason and an **Open case** link to `/admin/rent-agreement?request=<id>`; the **Feature** filter hides it under **Passes** and shows it under **Rent agreement**; an admin approves it there (gateway refund id set) and it moves to Approved. On the service desks **Closed** is completed only and the **Cancelled** tab holds cancelled/withdrawn cases. Backend `RentAgreementRefundTest.aCancelsRefundIsOnTheRefundsDesk` covers counts per feature, `feature=cars` → 400 and the decision note; `ServiceRequestQueueTest.queueSummaryCounts` covers `closed`/`cancelled`. | service-queues | ops/registration-check ("a cancelled agreement's refund is on the Refunds desk…"), ops/drafting-desk (tab list) | ✅ |
| **Pay before submit** (rent agreement hard stop). A direct-owner review shows "Pay ₹X & Submit". The request stays `awaiting-payment`, is shown as "Unpaid · not submitted", and is out of every staff queue until the signed payment webhook moves it to `new`. No tenant OTP step (tenant consent retired). Locally, a mock checkout offers a confirm that calls the `@LocalOnly` `POST /service-requests/{id}/payment/simulate`. Backend: `ServiceRequestFlowTest.PaidGate` (`invisibleToTheQueueUntilPaid`, `localPaymentSimulation`), `RentAgreementReadinessTest`. | services | consumer/services/rent-agreement-submit ("the owner pays before anything reaches the team…") | 🟡 |
| **Every executing party approves the current draft** (MC-4, V48). Account holders approve in-app; inline parties approve by OTP. A rejection sends the request to changes-requested, and a new version resets every approval. Backend `RentAgreementDraftApprovalTest`. | service-queues | ops/registration-check (`approvedAgreement` helper has the co-fill tenant approve) | ✅ |
| **A draft shared by one operator goes straight to the customer** (MC-8). A draft with rent ≥ ₹50k, a co-owner or POA, an NRI/foreign party or an overlap is visible and approvable by the customer the moment the holder shares it; there is no second-operator check, no `draftCheck` field and no `/draft/check` route. A revision shared while the request is already draft-shared is the current version at once (documents, `draftApproval`, open / decision / OTP all work). Two parties approving together still reach approved; a revision racing the last approval has one winner and never leaves the request approved on an unseen version. Backend `RentAgreementDraftApprovalTest` (`riskyDraftIsImmediatelyApprovable`, `reSharedRevisionIsImmediatelyApprovable`), `RentAgreementMakerCheckerRaceTest` | service-queues | ops/registration-check ("a high-rent draft shared by one operator reaches the customer at once", "the desk shows the deed particulars…") | ✅ |
| **Police intimation** (LEG-2). Automatic: registering on the IGR portal informs the police, so uploading the registered copy completes the desk's 7th stage ("All done") and the customer tracker shows "Police informed" with no portal links; there is no confirm endpoint or desk panel. Backend `RentAgreementRegistrationTest` (both cards read `done`). | service-queues | ops/registration-check ("registration completes police intimation with nothing for the desk or the customer to file") | ✅ |
| **A registered agreement lands on both dashboards** (RA-HOMES). Uploading the registered copy gives the owner a rented Owner Hub record (tenant, rent, lease end, the copy under a "Rent Agreement" vault category) and the tenant a tracked-rent card reading "Registered agreement till …" plus one Finance rental row. A savepoint keeps any failure from blocking completion. Backend `RentAgreementRegistrationTest` (`registeredAgreementSeedsBothDashboards`, `renewalRefreshesTheSameHome`, `listedFlatFillsItsExistingRecord`, `tenantStartedAgreementSeedsTheOwnerByMobile`) covers renewals updating the same record, a listed flat filling its existing record, and a tenant-started agreement seeding the owner matched by the OTP-approved mobile. | rent-agreement / owner-hub | ops/registration-check ("a registered agreement puts the flat on the owner's and the tenant's dashboards") | ✅ |
| **Rent loops after registration** (RA-RENT-LOOPS). The tenant taps "I've paid" and the card waits for the owner; the owner sees "{tenant} says {month} rent is paid" and either confirms (a receipt) or marks it not received (the claim is deleted and both sides are due again). Above ₹1 lakh a year the owner is prompted for their PAN; it is validated, saved uppercase and printed on receipts, and the tenant downloads a whole financial year as one PDF. Both cards show the deposit, "police informed" and a lease countdown. Backend `RentLoopTest` covers claim authz, the receipt outranking a claim, and claims older than the tenancy. **A tenant named after the month's due day owes from next month** (`firstRentMonth` on the owner's record): the owner card names that month instead of "Overdue" for this one, and the owner can confirm that early first rent as a receipt but no month beyond it (`RentLoopTest.ownerConfirmsAnEarlyFirstRent`). | owner-hub / dashboard | managed-properties ("the tenant says paid, the owner rejects then confirms, and the PAN reaches the FY receipts"), ops/registration-check ("a registered agreement puts the flat on the owner's and the tenant's dashboards") | ✅ |
| **Lease end brings both sides back** (RA-LEASE-END). Within 60 days of a registered lease ending, the owner's property page shows Renew (prefilled from the home via `GET /me/rent-agreements/homes/{id}/renewal`) and List/Relist, and the tenant's rental card shows Renew, Find next home (rent budget +20%), Book movers (move date prefilled) and an explicitly consented "Ask Draazy to call me". Settings carries a "Calls about homes and moving" switch (`marketingCalls`). Checked at 375px: 44px targets, no sideways scroll. Backend `LeaseEndTest` covers the reminder sweeper, renewal authz and publish availableOn. Staff follow-up of ending leases flows through the Leads CRM (lease-ending source). | owner-hub / dashboard / settings / ops | ops/registration-check ("a lease ending soon offers renewal to both sides on a phone"), platform/settings-preferences ("the marketing-calls consent switch is off by default and persists on the server") | ✅ |
| **Company, NRI and foreign parties** (LEG-1/LEG-3). A company or firm party is routed to the legal-desk quote flow. A foreign tenant is asked for a passport and visa/OCI instead of Aadhaar, sees the SRO-visit copy and the Form C (24h, indiafrro.gov.in) notice. | services | consumer/services/rent-agreement ("company or firm parties…", "a foreign tenant uses passport and visa details…") | ✅ |
| **Identity numbers after a refresh** (UX-7, D-g). Autosave keeps only markers; a restored draft shows a note naming the PAN fields to re-enter, and no number is in storage. | services | consumer/services/rent-agreement-validation ("a restored draft names the PAN fields…") | ✅ |
| **One invitation per tenant** (UX-6, D-j). Two tenant rows get two separate invitations (`party_index`); each invitee fills only their own row. | services | consumer/services/rent-agreement-cofill ("the requester can address two tenant rows…") | ✅ |
| **Send back for papers or an answer** (`action-required`). Only what staff cannot do themselves goes back: the holder picks owner or tenant, ticks that side's checklist papers to upload again and/or asks a question (Send back stays disabled with neither); the case shows **Sent back to the owner** with the papers named, and the next step turns to the customer. The asked side gets a `service.action-required` notification linking to `/services/rent-agreement`, a **Rent agreement** item ("Correction needed") in the dashboard **Needs your attention** panel that opens that page, and an amber **Needs your attention** banner with an upload per paper; **Done — send back to our team** stays disabled until every paper has a copy newer than the ask, then returns the case to `in-progress` with the reply in the thread. Non-holder 409, wrong caller 404, a second **Done** 409, a paper not on the asked side's checklist 422 and Done before the upload 409 are in `RentAgreementCorrectionsTest`. | service-queues | ops/rent-agreement-corrections ("the desk asks the owner for a fresh paper and an answer…") | ✅ |
| **The desk edits the form in place** (`PUT /service-requests/{id}/desk-details`). Every Licensor, Licensee, Property and Terms card has **Edit** for the holder; saving rewrites `details._state`, rebuilds the summary lines (`tenants`, `property`, `ownerName`) and tells the customer how many details changed. Passport, visa/OCI and FRRO fields are editable; list rows (furniture, deposit payments, occupants) can be added and removed. Priced terms are not on the form (a changed rent is a 409 pointing at **Priced terms**), PAN/Aadhaar go through **ID numbers**, and an unchanged form is 422, in `RentAgreementCorrectionsTest`. | service-queues | ops/rent-agreement-corrections ("the holder corrects a licensee card in place…", "the holder adds a furniture row the customer missed") | ✅ |
| **The desk corrects PAN / Aadhaar** (`PUT /service-requests/{id}/desk-identities`). On **ID numbers** the holder reveals the numbers, every party on the form is listed (recorded or not), and **Correct** saves a new PAN / Aadhaar for that slot; the customer is told an ID was corrected. Backend `RentAgreementCorrectionsTest.deskCorrectsIdentities`: the customer 403, a non-holder 409 (an admin too, until assigned), a slot not on the form 422, a repeat with the same numbers 422, other slots kept, and the audit row names the changed slot but carries no number; `deskRenameRelabelsTheNumbers`: a desk rename keeps that party's numbers under the new name. | service-queues | ops/rent-agreement-corrections ("the holder corrects a mistyped tenant PAN and Aadhaar…") | ✅ |
| **`/staff/refunds` and `/admin/refunds` — the Refunds desk.** Tabs Pending / Approved / Rejected / Automatic / Disputes with counts that equal the API's; risk-signal chips only on pending rows; Approve opens a confirm modal (Escape cancels, confirm approves, a repeat is 409); Reject needs a reason (blank disabled, server 422) and restores the entitlement. Staff without the `refunds` function and a customer are refused (403, and kept off the desk). **Read-only is simulated** by stripping `refunds:write` from the `/auth/me` and `/me/bootstrap` payloads, because the seeded roles bundle read and write in one function. Every row shows its payment (paid sum, order status, invoice); a pending one has no refund line yet. An Automatic row names its cause ("Paid after the order was cancelled or expired") and the refund's progress, from "Sent to Cashfree" with the Cashfree refund id to "Confirmed by Cashfree" once settled (the webhook outcome is written by SQL). Disputes are covered by counts only | settings-team-staff | ops/refunds-desk | ✅ |
| **`/staff/leads` and `/admin/leads` — the Leads CRM, on a 375px phone.** Staff sees an unowned lead in the **Common pool** (masked number, type chip "Buyer / Tenant · Rent", "via Walk-in" source, interest and consent chips; 44px Claim), claims it and finds it in My leads. Call/WhatsApp (owner only) dial or message the number and open an outcome sheet; saving "No answer" starts the 24h cooldown — "Next contact after …" shows, both buttons disable and a second call is 409 `lead-cooldown`. A second staffer finds the lead by name (min 3 chars), sees "Owned by …" with the number still masked in the list, opens it to the full number as a `tel:+91…` link ("+91 98xxx xxxxx") to dial from their own phone, has no Claim or Call, and a claim is 409 `lead-owned` — but can still edit the shared profile (property type, BHK, area, budget → "2 BHK · Flat / Apartment · Wakad · ₹25,000/mo"). The owner converts it on a paid service picked from the convertible list, and the manager's **Performance** tab (manager only, 7/30/90 days, a card per staff) shows the conversion credited to that owner. Add lead needs a lead type and a lead source before it enables; an Owner · Rent out lead via Facebook shows its profile line in the detail. Adding the same mobile again as a Buyer opens the existing lead ("Already a lead — opened it"), keeps Owner (re-add only fills blanks) and stores one row. The seeded flatmate "Arjun Mehta" sits in the pool under the Flatmate type filter and drops out under Owner. A buyer and a signed-out visitor land on `/staff-login`. Leads are seeded through `POST /admin/leads` as admin; the paid request is settled and `crm_leads.user_id` linked by SQL. | settings-team-staff | ops/leads-crm | ✅ |

## Notes on what this suite deliberately cannot reach

**What `i18n.spec.js` covers.** The app is English-only; i18next stays as the string catalogue and `npm run check:i18n` proves every static `t()` key resolves. The spec covers what a static scan cannot see: a key whose route namespace never loaded renders as a raw dotted path; helpers that return `{ key, count }` render `[object Object]` at a call site that forgets to translate; and a `dzLang` left in storage by the multilingual build must not switch anything.

**Stored ids stay English while labels translate.** Review categories, document categories, finance categories and ownership basis are persisted on user records, so translating an id would orphan every row saved against the old value — a data-migration bug wearing a copy-change costume. The module probes in that spec (`page.evaluate(() => import('/src/lib/...'))`) are unit assertions in an e2e costume and exist only to pin that id/label split. Two of the modules they reach into (`lib/data/finances.js`, `lib/qualityScore.js`) have no http provider yet and are rewritten in P5c; those assertions then move to wherever the ids come from, so leaving them here is a compile-time reminder rather than a silent coverage loss.

**`waitForLoadState('networkidle')` is not an app-readiness signal.** Vite fetches the whole module graph and only then evaluates it, so on a cold route the last response lands about a second before `main.jsx` runs (measured: last module +251ms, `networkidle` +839ms with `document.body.innerText.length === 0`, `main.jsx` +1181ms, first render +1702ms). The tap-target sweep ran against an empty document, found zero elements and asserted `[] === []`, passing for a screen it never saw. It is also unreachable after a client-side route change: a property detail page reached by clicking a card never produced 500ms of network silence inside 20s, while the same route reached by `goto()` passed.

`tests/mobile/tap-targets.spec.js:renderedSweep` replaced it with four gates: `data-dz-boot="ready"` set by `main.jsx`; a candidate-count floor (`MIN_CANDIDATES` 5, below the sparsest real screen at 7, far above the zero an unrendered document scores), re-asserted at measurement time so a route that renders and discards its subtree cannot slip through; two consecutive equal count readings, which asks directly whether anything is still arriving and cannot be defeated by a held-open request; and the absence of `<vite-error-overlay>`, because the app shell alone clears the floor, so a broken route looks exactly like a sparse one from a count. Keep the two polls separate — folded together, a real violation reports under the "never rendered" message, which names the wrong cause.

**A lazy-loading defect in a specification-backed read is unreachable from the Java suite.** `GET /admin/properties` shipped a `LazyInitializationException` on `owner` — the moderation queue returned 500 for every caller — while `PropertyModerationQueueTest` stayed green. The Java test is not thin (it covers status widening, `archived`, `recheck`, `q`, the 403 for a seeker and the 401 for an anonymous caller); but `AbstractApiTest` is `@Transactional`, so the persistence context stays open for the whole test method and a lazy proxy always resolves. An assertion on `content[0].owner.mobile` would pass with *and* without the fix, which is worse than no test. Reproducing it needs a non-transactional test that commits users and properties, and `draazy_test` must stay empty of domain data. So the guard is the live spec — the moderation-queue test in `property-integration` — protecting the join fetch in `PropertySpecs.adminSearch`, written the awkward way (`root.fetch` guarded on `!Long.class.equals(query.getResultType())`) because Spring Data issues a separate COUNT query where a join fetch is invalid SQL. Any future `JpaSpecificationExecutor` read that renders an association is in the same blind spot and belongs here rather than in the Java suite.

**Only the *settlement* half of the paid rent-agreement path is outside this suite.** `POST /service-requests` prices the request, parks it at `awaiting-payment` and returns a `paymentSessionId`; because `PaymentGateway` mints `mock_session_*` in dev and `lib/cashfree.js` returns early on that prefix, the checkout SDK never opens and the journey completes in-browser. So `consumer/services/rent-agreement` owns the create, the pricing, the park, the honest "payment outstanding" ending and the 409 on a second unpaid request. What remains backend-only: `ServiceRequestFlowTest.PaidGate` drives the real state machine — the ops queue does not show a parked request, a signature-verified webhook moves it to `new` (or cancels it on failure), redelivery is inert, an unrecognised `type` is a 400 rather than a free desk, and identity numbers in `details` are refused. Confirming that money actually moves needs the Cashfree sandbox, obtainable by hand on the deployed service but not from here: this lane runs against a local backend no callback can reach, signed with a key the suite must not hold.

**Two `no-gate` files divide by surface, not by duplication.** `list-property/no-gate` owns the wizard interior an owner lands in, while `flatmates/badge-not-gate` owns the flatmates board's three supply entry points (group form, room hand-off, seeker request) and the claim that granting the badge changes none of them. The rest of ADR-019's badge-not-gate claim is spread over `consumer/property/contact-badge-not-gate`, `consumer/flatmates/seeker-verify` (opt-in seeker badge), `platform/auth/kyc-growth-levers` (the opt-in badge funnel on the dashboard) and `platform/auth/verify-payoff` (what the badge actually buys).

**Half the verify payoff is backend-only.** A browser cannot earn a badge — the grant is a staff review decision — so the write onto an owner's listings is proven by `VerifiedOwnerListingsTest` and only the buyer-visible payoff stays in Playwright. Verification earns seven days of Featured placement and nothing else buys it: the reward is derived on read from `ownership_verified_at`, so `consumer/home/featured-reward` pins the clock edges and `property-ownership-review` the real grant and withdrawal, while `PropertiesEndpointsTest` and `PropertyModerationSummaryTest` pin it in Java, and `property-integration` asserts no console toggle or `toggle-featured` route survives.

**Legacy routes still have owners.** `/share-flat` redirects, and `consumer/flatmates/discovery` asserts the legacy `?view=` deep links resolve; it runs cross-viewport because the discovery surface differs on a phone. Shared fixtures live in `helpers/app.js` (`seed()`, `OWNER`, `SEEKER`, `ADMIN`, listing factories), and `playwright.config.js` routes viewports on `tests/mobile/**` rather than on a filename prefix, so the convention is visible in the layout.

## Gap backlog

Debt wave 10 shipped four user-visible frontend changes and **no e2e**, the only open backlog. Each is covered by a backend test or a build-time gate; the gap is that nothing drives them through a browser, where each would actually be wrong.

| Open gap | Why it is a gap, not a nicety |
|---|---|
| **Progressive disclosure on `/property/:id`** (D141) — four sections now start collapsed on mobile | `mobile/property-contact` scrolls to a fixed y-offset on this page and passes because the sticky bar is fixed, not because the content below it is right. A collapse that fails open costs nothing; one that fails **shut** silently hides amenities, and no assertion would notice. Needs a spec that expands each of the four and asserts the content arrives. |
| **The Turnstile widget on `/signin` and `/signup`** (D130) | The widget renders only when a site key is configured, so in mock mode it renders nothing and every existing auth spec passes either way. The failure worth catching is the opposite: a misconfigured key rendering a *blocking* widget on a form whose button is deliberately not gated. Also unexercised: the send waiting for an unfinished challenge, the widget remounting after a spend so Resend carries a fresh token, recovery from a failed script load, and the one retry on `bot_check_failed`. The wait/retry rules are unit-tested (`frontend/src/components/security/turnstileToken.test.mjs`) and siteverify's retry by `TurnstileBotDefenceTest`; the widget wiring needs Cloudflare's always-pass/always-fail test site keys in a browser. |
| **Report triage enforcement** (D68) — `actioned` + `hide_content` must take the listing down | Covered by `ReportService` tests server-side. No test covers the admin screen's half: that the enforcement control is *present*, that it is refused on a non-`actioned` decision, and that the dashboard's `openReports` tile agrees with the queue it links to. |
| **`buy.stamp_duty` renders as absent, not as ₹0** (D118 / V55) | `check:finance` proves the client's window matches the server's; it says nothing about what a null renders as. The point of the change was that a wrong number looked like a right one. |

### Closed, with what closing them found

The Phase-2 gaps and the seven items that followed are closed. Each finding below is a trap the next spec author can fall into again.

| Closed item | What the new spec found |
|---|---|
| `/services` hub → `consumer/services/services-hub` | Nothing broken, but the hub's `.reveal` blocks sit at `opacity: 0` until scrolled into view. `scrollIntoViewIfNeeded()` deadlocks on them (scrolling requires visibility); force `.visible` instead, as `consumer/property/detail-improvements` does. |
| `/locality/:slug` → `consumer/search/locality-intel` | Stats exist only with at least 3 live listings, so the busy-locality test looks one up at run time and skips when none exists. A freshly minted locality (`mintLocality`, placeId `mint-<slug>`) is the reliable "pending" case. The route is `/locality/:slug`, not `/localities/`. |
| `/pay-rent` → `pay-rent.spec.js` *(retired D256)* | Historical: `draazyDB_v5` could not be seeded in an init script (mockApi migrated and merged it at module load, leaving a blank page), so you loaded once, mutated the real DB, then navigated. The store is gone; the trap cannot recur. |
| `consumer/account/live-rent-payment-seam.spec.js` *(retired with V127)* | Covered the tenant→owner rent rail (`POST /me/rent-payments` opening a Cashfree order, the payment-method vocabulary, `/me/rent-ledger`, `/me/rent-mandate`, `/me/payout-account`); all four endpoints and three tables were dropped, so nothing is left to assert. Lesson for any future rail: the method assertion was "not 400 plus the exact `method must be one of` message" rather than 201, because the partial unique index answered the *second* valid method in a run with 409. |
| `/admin/localities` → `localities.spec.js` *(retired D256)* | `Table` renders a desktop `<table>` **and** a `.dz-card` list for phones, hiding one with CSS; unscoped text assertions trip strict mode or resolve to the hidden copy — **still true, and the one lesson that outlived the mock**. Historical: the seeded queue was **empty** (every `db.json` listing had a locality), and `draazyDB_v5` could not be written in an init script, so you navigated first and mutated after. Both stores are gone. |
| Help feedback → `platform/help/article-feedback` | Fine on the client branches; adding the server write found two bugs: the widget reset ran in an effect keyed on the slug, so a new article's first paint showed the *previous* one's thank-you card and support-link slug (fixed by keying the component; the spec navigates by **click**, since `goto` remounts and passes either way); and `useHelpLang()` returned i18next's raw `language`, which keeps a region tag (`en-US`) while resources resolve to `en`, so every affected POST would have been a 422 swallowed by the fire-and-forget catch. Normalised at the hook. |
| `DualRange` parsing → `consumer/search/dual-range-parsing` | Fine as shipped: `Cr`/`L`/`Lakh`/`Lac`/`K`, `₹`, commas and spaces all parse; unparseable input is ignored rather than coerced to 0. |
| Responsive pickers → `platform/date-time-fields`, `mobile/date-time-fields` | **Two real bugs.** (1) `.dz-datefield` / `.dz-cal` / `.dz-timepicker` lived in `styles/routes/list-property.css`, imported only by `ListProperty.jsx`, so on the other ~19 surfaces the field rendered as a plain block and the calendar as a static, unlayered element that reflowed the page. Moved to `styles/components/date-time-fields.css`, imported by `DateField.jsx` and `TimeField.jsx`. (2) `.dz-timepicker { width: 250px }` sat after the bottom-sheet block at equal specificity, so on a phone the time picker docked as a 250px stub against the left edge while the calendar went full width. Now guarded by `@media (min-width: 640px)`. |

**Measuring a fixed overlay.** `boundingBox()` is document-relative, so on a scrolled page it reports a docked sheet hundreds of pixels below the fold and fails against correct CSS. Read `getBoundingClientRect()` in `evaluate()`, and poll rather than sample once — the sheet animates up from `translateY(100%)`.

## Known-failing (pre-existing, product/spec drift)

**`consumer/connectivity.spec.js:145`** — *"a request that never reaches the server hedges"*, flaky (~1 run in 3), and the flakiness is the product's. `useConnectivity` holds one global `unreachable` latch that **any** successful request clears, including calls the failing screen never made, so the banner can flip from "Can't reach the server." to "Back online…" while the page still cannot load. Whether the assertion sees the right one is decided by request ordering.

Not noise: retiring the `lib/store.js` barrel moved the failure rate from **0/14 to 3/9** with no behaviour change (nothing imported the deleted modules; only Vite's chunking, and so request ordering, changed), measured by running the spec N times either side of a `git stash` — the only way to tell a new flake from an old one. The sibling at `:184` fails at the same rate on **both** sides and is genuinely pre-existing.

Not patched on purpose — widening the route interception or adding a retry would make the spec green while leaving the defect in place. Tracked as **D218**; the fix belongs in `useConnectivity`.

No other known-failing specs in this area. Three assertions in the old mock `types` spec under `consumer/list-property` had drifted from the wizard; all three were resolved when the folder moved to the live suite: the land case asserts what the product does (the 7/12 Extract is the ownership proof on offer, and the document block raises no error — the old spec demanded a mandatory-document error the wizard stopped emitting), while the MPCB and Agreement Duration cases were reachable and pass unchanged in `consumer/list-property/types`. The whole folder runs green against the live API.

Each ✅ spec asserts primary buttons/functions (happy path), the role/flag/team **guard** (redirect when unauthorized), an **empty state**, and any **maker-checker** transition.
